Delivering Life-Changing Value to Patients

                                                                                                      Patient Stories


                                                                                                      “I am currently living it moment by moment, and I am no longer the person who is
                                                                                                      afraid to confront people or who people looked at differently. I became a person who
                                                                                                      loves to see people and someone who loves to support them. Living a normal life on
"Commitment to Life"—woven through                                                                    the same planet, not shutting myself away from the world.”
­continuous dialogue between our employ-
                                                                                                                             Shahla from the United Arab Emirates (UAE), living with XLH
 ees and management at our founding in
 2008—is the guiding principle that flows
 through the heart of all our activities. Its
 essence lies in our unwavering sincerity
 toward "life" as a pharmaceutical company.
 We remain dedicated to our mission of
 ­delivering life-changing value to everyone
  battling illness, as we continue to push the
  boundaries of innovative drug discovery.

       Please refer to the inside back cover for the original text of "Commitment to Life."
       https://www.kyowakirin.com/index.html#modal-corevalue




                                                                                                                                                                                                         “We are deeply grateful and truly privileged to have witnessed what, in our eyes, feels
                                                                                                                                                                                                         like a remarkable change unfolding in our daughter’s journey.”

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                Luis Miguel Berlanas (Father of Candela, living with XLH)

                                                                                                                                                                                          any reference to the efficacy, safety, or clinical outcomes of any pharmaceutical products.

Directors

Against the backdrop of a rapidly changing business environment, Kyowa Kirin transitioned to a company with an Audit and Supervisory Committee, with the aim of establishing a business execution structure that balances transparency and agility. Our diverse management
team will vigorously promote our core business forward through swift and decisive decision-making, under the proper oversight of the Board of Directors.

Introduction to the C-suite Executive Framework

The newly formed C-suite Executive team, comprised of experts in various fields, will serve as a powerful driving force behind the Company’s sustained growth. Hailing from a variety of countries and backgrounds, this globally diverse team will play a leading role in driving
management forward by hammering out innovative strategies from multiple perspectives, thereby elevating the Group’s presence and influence in the global market to new heights.

                                                                                                                                                                 C-suite Executive
                                                                                                                                                 An Experienced Leadership Team with Global Diversity
                                                                                      Working to enhance the speed and quality of both the global management decision-making and executive functions, steps have been taken to evolve and implement a new manage-
                                                                                         ment execution structure for the Kyowa Kirin Group. Comprised of the Company’s Chairman, the CxOs, the Region Presidents, and the CEO of Orchard Therapeutics, the C-suite
                                                                                         Executive team forms part of Kyowa Kirin’s management. Team members are responsible for the Company’s overall decision-making function, transcending departmental and
                                                                                                                                        regional boundaries, and acting in the best interests of both internal and external stakeholders.

Foundation Through AI-Driven Operational Transformation
DX in operational processes addresses the fundamental challenge of balancing quality and produc-
tivity in the pharmaceutical industry, rather than focusing solely on efficiency. A representative
initiative is the company-wide cross-functional project ATOM.
  ATOM brought together teams from Sales, Compliance and Risk assessment, and Regulatory                                                                                                          Vision 2030
Affairs to tackle operational transformation using AI, starting from on-site challenges. By targeting
key processes—such as presentation slide reviews, material creation and review, and invitation
checks—we standardized and improved quality by codifying previously implicit decision criteria
and embedding them into AI.                                                                                             Where to focus
  A defining feature of ATOM is its use of AI not merely as an efficiency tool, but as a catalyst for
reexamining and redefining organizational standards and values. The “non-ambiguity” of AI triggered                                      Pipeline Expansion                                 Transformation of ways of working                                Patient-Centric Value Creation
open, cross-functional discussions, transforming relationships from “applicant versus reviewer” to a
one-team approach focused on shared agreement.
  As a result, psychological safety and consistency in decision-making have improved, alongside                         How to accelerate:
emerging benefits such as reduced workload and cost optimization. ATOM serves as a model case
demonstrating how DX can drive not only efficiency, but also collaboration and cultural evolution.

                                                                                                                                                                                                                   AI            and evolves workflows and processes

    Supporting Patients throughout Their Life Journey                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          Building upon the insights gained from this survey, Kyowa Kirin will continue its efforts to enhance information provision and support activities through collabora-
    At Kyowa Kirin, we are deeply committed to understanding and addressing the needs of patients at every stage of their lives.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               tion with patients, their families, and healthcare professionals.
        For patients with rare diseases, the challenges and concerns they face evolve significantly across different life stages—from childhood through adolescence to
    adulthood. Physical symptoms during growth, difficulties in school life, anxieties about social integration, and hopes for the future—each requires thoughtful
    attention and tailored support.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    For more information, please visit special website
        To help patients, families, and healthcare professionals better understand these complex patient experiences, we have developed Patient Journey Maps and                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       https://www.xlhimpact.com/
    animations for XLH and CTCL based on insights gathered from patient interviews, surveys, and extensive research. These maps visualize the physical, psychological,
    and social challenges faced in each life stage. Designed as accessible infographics, they serve as practical tools for healthcare professionals for shared decision-­
    making with patients and care partners, while also being easy                                                      Mapping the lived healthcare experiences of people with cutaneous t-cell lymphoma (CTCL; mycosis fungoides and sézary syndrome)
                                                                                                                      The challenge of living with CTCL doesn’t only come from uncomfortable symptoms; people with CTCL also struggle with the emotional burden of their condition. The emotional burden of CTCL can evolve over time, from getting a diagnosis to
                                                                                                                       treatment, and beyond. Using insights from patient interviews, surveys and desk research, we’ve mapped how the feelings of people with CTCL can change over the course of their disease, and what unmet needs still exist in this community. Orchard Therapeutics’ Libmeldy®/Lenmeldy® targets metachromatic leukodystrophy (MLD), a disease that does not show any particu-
            For more information, please visit Kyowa Kirin International (EMEA) website                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        lar symptoms immediately after birth, yet progresses rapidly following the onset of symptoms in the case of onset at infancy. Given that this disease can have a
            https://kyowakirininternational.com/what-we-do/supporting-patients/                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                major impact on life, it is therefore critical to diagnose it at the earliest possible stage before onset, and in particular immediately after birth. In order to save as
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               many MLD patients as possible, Orchard Therapeutics supports activities by governments and related academic societies that seek to include MLD testing as part of
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               neonatal screening in each country, in an effort to help identify patients early on and to improve opportunities to provide treatment.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                addition of MLD to the Norwegian Newborn Screening (NBS) program in 2025, Sweden has published a national recommendation to include MLD in its screening

   directors of the Board and one Audit & Supervisory Board member to whom no remuneration was paid.
*2 All the amounts of performance-linked annual bonus, share-based remuneration with restriction on transfer, and performance-linked share-based remuneration are the amounts recorded as expenses during the fiscal year under review. The amount of performance-­
   linked share-based remuneration is the sum of the amounts recorded as expenses during FY2025 for each of performance-linked share-based remuneration with the performance evaluation period starting in FY2024 and FY2025 in accordance with the expected
   degree of achievement of the targets as of the end of the fiscal year. Performance-linked share-based remuneration is paid and delivered in the form of monetary and non-monetary remuneration after the elapse of the performance evaluation period.

   closing price on March 28, 2025).
*4 “Other remuneration” is collateral remuneration incurred in association with stays in Japan, resolved at Board of Directors’ meetings after deliberations at the Nomination & Remuneration Consultative Committee, including Company housing expenses, temporary

Risk Management

To earn the trust of its customers and society, the Kyowa Kirin Group identifies and responds appropriately to the various risks that arise in conducting its business activities.


Kyowa Kirin Group Risk Management                                                                         Crisis Management Structure and Enhancement of BCP Exercises                                            enhancements, the Group has established a resilient framework that consistently covers crisis
At the Kyowa Kirin Group, risk is defined as uncertainty that may affect the achievement of manage-       Among events that may impede the achievement of management objectives, those requiring                  prevention, preparedness, early detection of warning signs, and emergency response. In addition,
ment and strategic objectives. This includes not only threats that could negatively impact corporate      urgent response due to their significant impact are defined as “crises,” and activities undertaken to   focusing on material risks, the Group conducts cross-regional and cross-functional crisis and BCP
value in the conduct of business, but also opportunities that, if appropriately addressed, may con-       minimize their impact once risks materialize into crises are defined as “crisis management.”            exercises to strengthen crisis response capabilities and business continuity under worst-case
tribute to value creation and sustainable growth.                                                             In the event of a crisis, the Kyowa Kirin Group implements swift and structured crisis manage-      scenarios. Under the revised policy, training and drills are, in principle, conducted at least once a
    Under the Enterprise Risk Management (ERM) framework, risks are not viewed merely as matters          ment in accordance with the Group Crisis Management Policy (revised on November 11, 2025). This         year by each response headquarters, followed by post-exercise reviews, formulation of recurrence
to be avoided, but as critical management considerations that should be addressed in an integrated        is achieved through close coordination between Function-based Response Headquarters, which              prevention measures, development of improvement action plans, and continuous improvement
manner with strategy. Through protecting corporate value from potential threats while at the same         enable an early transition from normal operations to crisis response, and Global, Regional, and Local   during normal operations.
time enabling appropriate risk-taking to capture opportunities, the Group implements risk manage-         Response Headquarters established depending on the geographical scope of the crisis.                        Through these exercises, the Group not only enhances its response capabilities but also revisits
ment with the aim of generating new corporate value. realization of our Vision and the achievement of our management plans.                                    reporting lines, and escalation principles (including “Bad News Fast”) for each level—Global,           signals.                                                                                                           Regional, Local, and Function-based. Risk Factors

This section describes principal risks identified by the Kyowa Kirin Group as of December 31, 2025. However, the Group may face other unforeseen risks caused by changes in internal and external conditions,
and risks not described here may have a negative impact on the Group’s business performance and financial position.
The Group defines risks as factors that could have an uncertain impact on business targets, including both threats and opportunities.
